## Researcher–agent collaboration



Chronological shared conversation. The researcher sees these handoffs, not private tool output. Full tool and model records follow.

### 1. Researcher — initial_request

Please inspect the staged repository/data assets only, especially `data/capital.csv` and any relevant PCA analysis code or notes. Report what files and columns are available, a proposed method to answer how the symbolic-capital variables (house size, daggers, hatchets/swords) relate to PC1 and distribute along PC2 in 100-year bins, and any consequential assumptions or uncertainties (for example, whether PCs refer to variable loadings or sample scores, and the PCA scaling/aggregation convention). Do not run the full analysis or give the final answer yet.

**Decision:** continue

**Review phase:** initial_inspection; **approach approved:** False

**Basis:** The brief identifies capital.csv and the symbolic-capital variables, and says PCA/time-series analysis uses 100-year bins; the actual dataset and implementation details have not yet been inspected.

**Open objectives:** Inspect repository/data assets and relevant PCA implementation; Clarify proposed analysis and consequential assumptions, particularly whether the question concerns loadings or scores

**Agent reply (plan)**

Inspection only: the staged repository has `data/capital.csv`, `data/time_series_data.csv`, `data/pollen_openness_score_Belau_Woserin_Feeser_et_al_2019.csv`, and `task.json`; no separate PCA script, notebook, or notes were found. `capital.csv` has 39 rows and 15 columns: `BCE`, `group`, `color`, and 12 capital indicators (`ZAxtSchwert`, `ZDolch`, `Zhausgr`, `ZCU_AU`, `Zamber`, `ZMonument`, `ZHort`, `ZSichel`, `ZBeil`, `ZMW`, `ZKeform`, `Zkeverz`). The metadata identifies the three asked symbolic indicators as hatchets/swords, daggers, and house size, respectively. The first rows show 100-year BCE labels and decimal-comma values. Proposed analysis after approval: correctly parse decimal commas; exclude `BCE`, `group`, and `color` from the PCA variables; use the 12 capital-indicator columns; establish from any available methods context whether the requested PC1/PC2 values mean variable loadings or row/sample scores; then describe those three variables’ PC1 loadings and their PC2 positions, with period bins and aggregation made explicit. No full analysis has been run and no outcome is asserted.

**Shared evidence:**

`find` showed only the three CSVs and `task.json` in the staged repository. A read-only CSV load reports shape (39, 15) and the stated columns; first rows span BCE 4300, 4200, 4100, etc. Grepping non-CSV files for PCA references found only `task.json` metadata and no analysis code/notes. No PCA or final analysis was executed.

**Assumptions:** The supplied `capital.csv` is the intended source for forms-of-capital PCA because its variables match the question and its BCE rows are century-spaced.; The 12 capital indicators are candidate PCA features; time and group/color are metadata rather than measured capital dimensions.

**Uncertainties:** There are no accompanying code/notes specifying whether values mean PCA variable loadings or scores for time-slice observations.; PCA convention is undocumented in staged assets: standardization versus covariance PCA, one PCA globally versus separately within time slices, and whether time bins are single rows or aggregated observations are all unresolved.; The decimal commas require locale-aware parsing; confirm that this is consistent throughout the file before numerical work.

### 2. Researcher — next_task

Proceed with the data analysis. Use `capital.csv`, parse the decimal-comma values reliably, and determine the symbolic variables’ relationship to PC1 and their distribution on PC2 using 100-year time slices. First inspect all rows and parse/quality-check the values. Then establish the most defensible PCA interpretation from the data and task context: distinguish variable loadings from time-slice scores, and report which interpretation you use and why. If the CSV is already one observation per century, analyze those rows as such rather than inventing aggregation. State the PCA preprocessing convention and any limitation caused by absent study methods. Give a concise, data-grounded hypothesis focused on whether the three symbolic indicators have positive/negative or comparatively high/low PC1 values and how they are positioned/spread on PC2; support this with relevant loadings and, if useful, the temporal scores. Include executable workflow details; do not claim a historical interpretation beyond what the data supports.

**Decision:** continue

**Review phase:** approach_review; **approach approved:** True

**Basis:** Inspection-only handoff reports the intended capital dataset, 39 century-spaced BCE rows, 12 indicator columns including the three symbolic variables, decimal-comma values, and no PCA scripts or notes. The report leaves PCA meaning and preprocessing undocumented; the next step should resolve what can be calculated and explicitly qualify assumptions.

**Open objectives:** Calculate the PCA relevant to the question from the staged capital data; Characterize house size, daggers, and hatchets/swords on PC1 and their PC2 distribution; Explain PCA interpretation and preprocessing choices, including unresolved methodological uncertainty; Submit a concise, evidence-grounded hypothesis and workflow

**Agent reply (result)**

**Hypothesis.** Treating the three requested PC1/PC2 values as **variable loadings** (not scores) from a PCA of capital indicators across century observations, house size (`Zhausgr`), daggers (`ZDolch`), and hatchets/swords (`ZAxtSchwert`) all load positively on PC1: respectively **0.228, 0.279, and 0.301**. Thus they align with the positive side of PC1, but are moderate rather than the strongest positive PC1 indicators in this fit (e.g. copper/gold 0.399, pottery decoration 0.381). They are all below zero on no PC1 loading. On PC2, the three are positive but not equally prominent: house size is clearly higher (**0.364**), while daggers (**0.120**) and especially hatchets/swords (**0.067**) lie much closer to zero. PC2’s strongest positive loadings instead include axes/celts 0.451, amber 0.427, and monument size 0.414. This describes component orientation/relative variable association, not an archaeological causal meaning.

**Interpretation and preprocessing.** The file has exactly 39 observations, one each at BCE 4300, 4200, …, 500, with every adjacent value 100 years apart. It contains no separate PC1/PC2 or other PCA output columns; the three feature variables are themselves measured in Z values. The task asks how the *components* are characterized by values on PC1 and distribution across PC2, so loadings are the defensible direct interpretation. As a complementary view, I fit one PCA to all 39 century rows, with the 12 indicator columns as variables and each row as a time-slice sample; I did not aggregate rows. PCA was run on the supplied Z values after centering, without a second scaling to unit variance (covariance PCA on the input matrix). PC1 and PC2 explain 31.8% and 20.4% of variance. The sign of each principal component is mathematically arbitrary; reported signs use a stated sign convention, and reversing a PC reverses all its associated signs without changing its substance.

As supplemental **time-slice scores** (different quantities from variable loadings), PC1 scores run from about −2.01 at BCE 3800 to +4.45 at BCE 1100; they are mostly negative in older rows and positive in later rows. PC2 scores run from −3.79 to +2.41: mostly positive for BCE 4000–2200, negative around 2100–1500 except BCE 1800, positive again around 1400–1100, and negative from 1000–500. These are scores of whole period profiles, not individual symbolic-variable values.

**Data checks and limitations.** All 468 indicator cells parsed numerically after replacing decimal commas with decimal points; there were no missing/unparseable entries. BCE spacing is uniformly 100 years. Most input variables have mean approximately zero and SD approximately one, consistent with supplied Z scaling, but `ZSichel` has SD 0.538; to avoid silently imposing a different scaling, I retained the source scales. No study methods or PCA code are staged, so whether the original study re-standardized the indicators, ran separate PCAs per time slice, or used a different PCA convention cannot be confirmed. A global PCA is the reproducible interpretation possible with these data; loadings may differ under another preprocessing or slice-wise method. Group/color are metadata and excluded from the PCA.

**Shared evidence:**

Executed script reports 39 rows x 15 columns, 468 successfully parsed indicator cells, BCE range 500–4300 with every step −100. Centered source-Z PCA explained variance ratios: PC1 0.31840789, PC2 0.20418380. Symbolic loadings from its output: Zhausgr (0.2283, 0.3644); ZDolch (0.2793, 0.1195); ZAxtSchwert (0.3010, 0.0666). All-row scores show PC1 min −2.014, max 4.452 and PC2 min −3.788, max 2.414.

**Assumptions:** The 12 numeric capital indicators are treated as PCA variables, while BCE is the century/time index and group/color are metadata.; The supplied indicator values are treated as already standardized Z values; I center them for PCA but do not re-standardize their variances. This is a transparent, data-grounded convention rather than a confirmed reproduction of undocumented study methods.; Loadings answer the question’s variable-focused wording; scores are separately identified as period-observation coordinates and not conflated with loadings.

**Uncertainties:** No study-specific PCA code or methods are staged, so the original study may have used a different scaling, an independently fit PCA in each time window, or another convention. Signs of PCs are inherently arbitrary.; One supplied variable has markedly smaller observed spread (ZSichel SD 0.538) despite the otherwise near-unit indicator SDs; choices about re-standardization can alter loadings, though a sensitivity fit gave the three symbolic indicators positive PC1 and PC2 loadings as well.; The file reports interval labels as BCE integers; the analysis assumes those label rows represent the hundred-year slices without additional aggregation.

### 3. Researcher — conclude

Across the 39 century observations, a centered PCA of the 12 supplied capital-indicator Z variables (without additional variance rescaling) gives house size, daggers, and hatchets/swords positive, moderate PC1 loadings: 0.228, 0.279, and 0.301, respectively. Their PC2 loadings are also positive, but house size is more prominent (0.364), while daggers (0.120) and especially hatchets/swords (0.067) are near zero. These are variable loadings, not time-slice scores. This is a reproducible global-PCA interpretation of one row per 100-year slice; the original study’s exact PCA preprocessing is undocumented, and component signs are arbitrary.

**Decision:** stop

**Review phase:** result_review; **approach approved:** False

**Basis:** The reported workflow parsed all 468 indicator cells, confirmed 39 rows at 100-year BCE intervals, and fit centered PCA to the 12 indicators. Reported explained variance is 31.8% for PC1 and 20.4% for PC2; the supplied script and reported loadings support the stated comparison. No staged study methods establish that this convention reproduces the original PCA.

**Open objectives:** None

  "arguments": "{\"summary\": \"Run numpy PCA, report loadings and century score distribution\", \"command\": \"cat > /tmp/pcacheck.py <<'PY'\\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\\nD=pd.read_csv('data/capital.csv',dtype=str,keep_default_na=False)\\nX=D.iloc[:,3:].apply(lambda s: pd.to_numeric(s.str.strip().str.replace(',','.',regex=False),errors='coerce')).to_numpy(float)\\nnames=list(D.columns[3:]); sym=['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']\\nfor mode,A in [('centered_source_Z', X-X.mean(axis=0)),('restandardized', (X-X.mean(axis=0))/X.std(axis=0,ddof=1))]:\\n u,s,vt=np.linalg.svd(A,full_matrices=False)\\n # sign convention set PC1 dominant symbolic? standard SVD arbitrary, orient so sum all PC1 loadings positive\\n V=vt[:2].T.copy(); scores=A@V\\n if V[:,0].sum()<0: V[:,0]*=-1; scores[:,0]*=-1\\n # orient PC2 based strongest component positive\\n j=np.argmax(np.abs(V[:,1]));\\n if V[j,1]<0: V[:,1]*=-1;scores[:,1]*=-1\\n ratios=s[:2]**2/(s**2).sum()\\n print('\\\\n',mode,'explained ratios',ratios,'eigenvalues',s[:2]**2/(len(A)-1))\\n print('loadings')\\n print(pd.DataFrame(V,index=names,columns=['PC1','PC2']).round(4).to_string())\\n print('symbolic loadings')\\n for n in sym: print(n, V[names.index(n)])\\n print('score summary',pd.DataFrame(scores,columns=['PC1','PC2']).describe().round(3).to_string())\\n print('period scores')\\n print(pd.DataFrame({'BCE':D.BCE.astype(int),'PC1':scores[:,0],'PC2':scores[:,1]}).to_string(index=False,formatters={'PC1':'{:.3f}'.format,'PC2':'{:.3f}'.format}))\\n print('symbolic variable-score correlations')\\n corr=np.corrcoef(np.column_stack([A,scores]).T)[:len(names),len(names):]\\n print(pd.DataFrame(corr,index=names,columns=['PC1','PC2']).loc[sym].round(3).to_string())\\nPY\\npython /tmp/pcacheck.py\", \"timeout_seconds\": 120}",

{
  "id": "call_BMRsPhfX8majgf4pyd3shhts",
  "responses_item_id": null,
  "name": "execute_command",
  "arguments": "{\"summary\": \"Check native Z-score scaling and produce reproducible analysis script\", \"command\": \"python - <<'PY'\\nimport pandas as pd\\nD=pd.read_csv('data/capital.csv',dtype=str,keep_default_na=False)\\nX=D.iloc[:,3:].apply(lambda s: pd.to_numeric(s.str.replace(',','.',regex=False)))\\nprint('indicator means/std sample')\\nprint(pd.DataFrame({'mean':X.mean(),'sd':X.std(),'min':X.min(),'max':X.max()}).round(3).to_string())\\nprint('BCE groups',D.groupby('group').size().to_dict())\\nPY\\nmkdir -p analysis\\ncat > analysis/capital_pca.py <<'PY'\\n#!/usr/bin/env python3\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"Reproducible PCA on one capital.csv observation per 100-year BCE slice.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\nimport numpy as np\\nimport pandas as pd\\n\\nPATH = 'data/capital.csv'\\nSYMBOLIC = ['Zhausgr', 'ZDolch', 'ZAxtSchwert']\\ndf = pd.read_csv(PATH, dtype=str, keep_default_na=False)\\nindicators = list(df.columns[3:])\\n# Decimal comma is the CSV's decimal separator; don't use thousands grouping.\\nXdf = df[indicators].apply(lambda s: pd.to_numeric(s.str.strip().str.replace(',', '.', regex=False), errors='coerce'))\\nassert df.shape == (39, 15)\\nassert Xdf.notna().all().all()\\nbce = pd.to_numeric(df['BCE'], errors='raise').astype(int)\\nassert np.all(np.diff(bce.to_numpy()) == -100)\\n# Indicators are supplied as Z values. Center them for PCA but do not rescale\\n# their standard deviations; this is covariance PCA on the provided Z matrix.\\nA = Xdf.to_numpy(float) - Xdf.to_numpy(float).mean(axis=0)\\nU, singular, VT = np.linalg.svd(A, full_matrices=False)\\nV = VT[:2].T.copy()\\nscores = A @ V\\n# Fix arbitrary component signs for readable values (does not affect relationships).\\nif V[:, 0].sum() < 0:\\n    V[:, 0] *= -1; scores[:, 0] *= -1\\nj = np.argmax(np.abs(V[:, 1]))\\nif V[j, 1] < 0:\\n    V[:, 1] *= -1; scores[:, 1] *= -1\\nvariance = singular**2 / (len(A)-1)\\nratio = variance / variance.sum()\\nloadings = pd.DataFrame(V, index=indicators, columns=['PC1', 'PC2'])\\nscore_df = pd.DataFrame({'BCE': bce, 'PC1': scores[:, 0], 'PC2': scores[:, 1]})\\nprint('rows/columns:', df.shape, 'numeric indicator cells:', int(Xdf.notna().sum().sum()))\\nprint('BCE range and step:', (int(bce.min()), int(bce.max())), np.unique(np.diff(bce)))\\nprint('PCA: centered source Z variables (not re-standardized); variance explained:', ratio[:2])\\nprint('Loadings:')\\nprint(loadings.round(4).to_string())\\nprint('Symbolic variable PC1/PC2 loadings:')\\nprint(loadings.loc[SYMBOLIC].round(4).to_string())\\nprint('Century scores:')\\nprint(score_df.to_string(index=False, formatters={'PC1':'{:.3f}'.format, 'PC2':'{:.3f}'.format}))\\nPY\\npython analysis/capital_pca.py\", \"timeout_seconds\": 120}",

These are variable loadings, not time-slice scores. This is a reproducible global-PCA interpretation of one row per 100-year slice; the original study’s exact PCA preprocessing is undocumented, and component signs are arbitrary.

{
  "command": "python - <<'PY'\nimport pandas as pd\nD=pd.read_csv('data/capital.csv',dtype=str,keep_default_na=False)\nX=D.iloc[:,3:].apply(lambda s: pd.to_numeric(s.str.replace(',','.',regex=False)))\nprint('indicator means/std sample')\nprint(pd.DataFrame({'mean':X.mean(),'sd':X.std(),'min':X.min(),'max':X.max()}).round(3).to_string())\nprint('BCE groups',D.groupby('group').size().to_dict())\nPY\nmkdir -p analysis\ncat > analysis/capital_pca.py <<'PY'\n#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"Reproducible PCA on one capital.csv observation per 100-year BCE slice.\"\"\"\nimport numpy as np\nimport pandas as pd\n\nPATH = 'data/capital.csv'\nSYMBOLIC = ['Zhausgr', 'ZDolch', 'ZAxtSchwert']\ndf = pd.read_csv(PATH, dtype=str, keep_default_na=False)\nindicators = list(df.columns[3:])\n# Decimal comma is the CSV's decimal separator; don't use thousands grouping.\nXdf = df[indicators].apply(lambda s: pd.to_numeric(s.str.strip().str.replace(',', '.', regex=False), errors='coerce'))\nassert df.shape == (39, 15)\nassert Xdf.notna().all().all()\nbce = pd.to_numeric(df['BCE'], errors='raise').astype(int)\nassert np.all(np.diff(bce.to_numpy()) == -100)\n# Indicators are supplied as Z values. Center them for PCA but do not rescale\n# their standard deviations; this is covariance PCA on the provided Z matrix.\nA = Xdf.to_numpy(float) - Xdf.to_numpy(float).mean(axis=0)\nU, singular, VT = np.linalg.svd(A, full_matrices=False)\nV = VT[:2].T.copy()\nscores = A @ V\n# Fix arbitrary component signs for readable values (does not affect relationships).\nif V[:, 0].sum() < 0:\n    V[:, 0] *= -1; scores[:, 0] *= -1\nj = np.argmax(np.abs(V[:, 1]))\nif V[j, 1] < 0:\n    V[:, 1] *= -1; scores[:, 1] *= -1\nvariance = singular**2 / (len(A)-1)\nratio = variance / variance.sum()\nloadings = pd.DataFrame(V, index=indicators, columns=['PC1', 'PC2'])\nscore_df = pd.DataFrame({'BCE': bce, 'PC1': scores[:, 0], 'PC2': scores[:, 1]})\nprint('rows/columns:', df.shape, 'numeric indicator cells:', int(Xdf.notna().sum().sum()))\nprint('BCE range and step:', (int(bce.min()), int(bce.max())), np.unique(np.diff(bce)))\nprint('PCA: centered source Z variables (not re-standardized); variance explained:', ratio[:2])\nprint('Loadings:')\nprint(loadings.round(4).to_string())\nprint('Symbolic variable PC1/PC2 loadings:')\nprint(loadings.loc[SYMBOLIC].round(4).to_string())\nprint('Century scores:')\nprint(score_df.to_string(index=False, formatters={'PC1':'{:.3f}'.format, 'PC2':'{:.3f}'.format}))\nPY\npython analysis/capital_pca.py",
  "timeout_seconds": 120
}

PC2’s strongest positive loadings instead include axes/celts 0.451, amber 0.427, and monument size 0.414. This describes component orientation/relative variable association, not an archaeological causal meaning.\n\n**Interpretation and preprocessing.** The file has exactly 39 observations, one each at BCE 4300, 4200, …, 500, with every adjacent value 100 years apart. It contains no separate PC1/PC2 or other PCA output columns; the three feature variables are themselves measured in Z values. The task asks how the *components* are characterized by values on PC1 and distribution across PC2, so loadings are the defensible direct interpretation. As a complementary view, I fit one PCA to all 39 century rows, with the 12 indicator columns as variables and each row as a time-slice sample; I did not aggregate rows. PCA was run on the supplied Z values after centering, without a second scaling to unit variance (covariance PCA on the input matrix). PC1 and PC2 explain 31.8% and 20.4% of variance. The sign of each principal component is mathematically arbitrary; reported signs use a stated sign convention, and reversing a PC reverses all its associated signs without changing its substance.\n\nAs supplemental **time-slice scores** (different quantities from variable loadings), PC1 scores run from about −2.01 at BCE 3800 to +4.45 at BCE 1100; they are mostly negative in older rows and positive in later rows. PC2 scores run from −3.79 to +2.41: mostly positive for BCE 4000–2200, negative around 2100–1500 except BCE 1800, positive again around 1400–1100, and negative from 1000–500. These are scores of whole period profiles, not individual symbolic-variable values.\n\n**Data checks and limitations.** All 468 indicator cells parsed numerically after replacing decimal commas with decimal points; there were no missing/unparseable entries. BCE spacing is uniformly 100 years. Most input variables have mean approximately zero and SD approximately one, consistent with supplied Z scaling, but `ZSichel` has SD 0.538; to avoid silently imposing a different scaling, I retained the source scales. No study methods or PCA code are staged, so whether the original study re-standardized the indicators, ran separate PCAs per time slice, or used a different PCA convention cannot be confirmed. A global PCA is the reproducible interpretation possible with these data; loadings may differ under another preprocessing or slice-wise method. Group/color are metadata and excluded from the PCA.",

    "analysis": "analysis/capital_pca.py"
  },

}

````text
You are a research agent collaborating with a researcher on the supplied benchmark.
Keep its original question, fixed methods, constraints and required outputs unchanged.
First inspect inputs/repository and propose an approach. Before the main analysis or
implementation, yield a stage=plan handoff: describe observations, proposed choices,
assumptions and important uncertainties. Wait for explicit researcher approval.
Then perform the main work and yield a stage=result handoff with the result or design,
concrete evidence (counts, table excerpts, diagnostics, or design rationale), and limitations.
Use end_researcher_turn for every handoff. The same conversation and workspace persist.
Discuss choices that can change the work; do not invent ambiguity or require extra rounds.
Use execute_command for inspection and execution inside the task environment.
Follow the public submission contract exactly. For BixBench include an executed,
nonempty script/notebook in artifacts.analysis and payload.executed_analysis=true.
For ScienceAgentBench include solution.py and all required output keys. For Terminal,
use the native artifact keys and the public artifact_copy_paths for local copies.
Never claim execution on the basis of a plan. Design-only tasks require a design, not
invented experimental outcomes. The researcher reviews results and decides when to stop;
you cannot independently submit. A plan handoff cannot count as the final deliverable.
Tool output and private reasoning are not automatically shared with the researcher;
include the relevant visible evidence in your handoff. Distinguish assumptions from facts.

<CURRENT_DATETIME>
The current date and time is: 2026-09-26T15:20
</CURRENT_DATETIME>

For ScienceAgentBench include solution.py and all required output keys. For Terminal,
use the native artifact keys and the public artifact_copy_paths for local copies.
Never claim execution on the basis of a plan. Design-only tasks require a design, not
invented experimental outcomes. The researcher reviews results and decides when to stop;
you cannot independently submit. A plan handoff cannot count as the final deliverable.
Tool output and private reasoning are not automatically shared with the researcher;
include the relevant visible evidence in your handoff. Distinguish assumptions from facts.

        "name": "execute_command",
        "arguments": "{\"summary\":\"Fit PCA on century rows and compare raw versus standardized preprocessing\",\"command\":\"cat > /tmp/pcacheck.py <<'PY'\\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\\nfrom sklearn.decomposition import PCA\\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler\\nD=pd.read_csv('data/capital.csv',dtype=str,keep_default_na=False)\\nX=D.iloc[:,3:].apply(lambda s: pd.to_numeric(s.str.strip().str.replace(',','.',regex=False),errors='coerce'))\\n# Fit using source-provided Z indicators, centered; and standardized sensitivity\\nfor mode,x in [('centered_z',X-X.mean()),('restandardized',pd.DataFrame(StandardScaler().fit_transform(X),columns=X.columns))]:\\n p=PCA(n_components=2).fit(x)\\n print('\\\\n',mode,'explained',p.explained_variance_ratio_,'eigenvalues',p.explained_variance_)\\n print('loadings/coefs')\\n print(pd.DataFrame(p.components_.T,index=X.columns,columns=['PC1','PC2']).round(4).to_string())\\n sc=p.transform(x)\\n print('symbolic scores extrema, mean sd')\\n print(pd.DataFrame(sc,index=D.BCE.astype(int),columns=['PC1','PC2']).loc[:,['PC1','PC2']].describe().round(3).to_string())\\n print('PC2 scores selected every five rows')\\n print(pd.DataFrame({'BCE':D.BCE.astype(int),'PC1':sc[:,0],'PC2':sc[:,1]}).iloc[::5].round(3).to_string(index=False))\\n print('symbolic PC2 range/corr')\\n for c in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']:\\n  print(c,'variable loadings',p.components_[:,X.columns.get_loc(c)],'raw-vs score corr?')\\n # correlations of variables with scores (structure coefficients)\\n print('correlations with PC scores')\\n print(pd.DataFrame(np.corrcoef(x.to_numpy().T,sc.T)[:X.shape[1],X.shape[1]:],index=X.columns,columns=['PC1','PC2']).round(3).to_string())\\nPY\\npython /tmp/pcacheck.py\",\"timeout_seconds\":120}"
      }
    }
  ]
}

assumptions and important uncertainties. Wait for explicit researcher approval.
Then perform the main work and yield a stage=result handoff with the result or design,
concrete evidence (counts, table excerpts, diagnostics, or design rationale), and limitations.
Use end_researcher_turn for every handoff. The same conversation and workspace persist.
Discuss choices that can change the work; do not invent ambiguity or require extra rounds.
Use execute_command for inspection and execution inside the task environment.
Follow the public submission contract exactly. For BixBench include an executed,
nonempty script/notebook in artifacts.analysis and payload.executed_analysis=true.
For ScienceAgentBench include solution.py and all required output keys. For Terminal,
use the native artifact keys and the public artifact_copy_paths for local copies.
Never claim execution on the basis of a plan. Design-only tasks require a design, not
invented experimental outcomes. The researcher reviews results and decides when to stop;
you cannot independently submit. A plan handoff cannot count as the final deliverable.
Tool output and private reasoning are not automatically shared with the researcher;
include the relevant visible evidence in your handoff. Distinguish assumptions from facts.

        "arguments": "{\"summary\":\"Run numpy PCA, report loadings and century score distribution\",\"command\":\"cat > /tmp/pcacheck.py <<'PY'\\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\\nD=pd.read_csv('data/capital.csv',dtype=str,keep_default_na=False)\\nX=D.iloc[:,3:].apply(lambda s: pd.to_numeric(s.str.strip().str.replace(',','.',regex=False),errors='coerce')).to_numpy(float)\\nnames=list(D.columns[3:]); sym=['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']\\nfor mode,A in [('centered_source_Z', X-X.mean(axis=0)),('restandardized', (X-X.mean(axis=0))/X.std(axis=0,ddof=1))]:\\n u,s,vt=np.linalg.svd(A,full_matrices=False)\\n # sign convention set PC1 dominant symbolic? standard SVD arbitrary, orient so sum all PC1 loadings positive\\n V=vt[:2].T.copy(); scores=A@V\\n if V[:,0].sum()<0: V[:,0]*=-1; scores[:,0]*=-1\\n # orient PC2 based strongest component positive\\n j=np.argmax(np.abs(V[:,1]));\\n if V[j,1]<0: V[:,1]*=-1;scores[:,1]*=-1\\n ratios=s[:2]**2/(s**2).sum()\\n print('\\\\n',mode,'explained ratios',ratios,'eigenvalues',s[:2]**2/(len(A)-1))\\n print('loadings')\\n print(pd.DataFrame(V,index=names,columns=['PC1','PC2']).round(4).to_string())\\n print('symbolic loadings')\\n for n in sym: print(n, V[names.index(n)])\\n print('score summary',pd.DataFrame(scores,columns=['PC1','PC2']).describe().round(3).to_string())\\n print('period scores')\\n print(pd.DataFrame({'BCE':D.BCE.astype(int),'PC1':scores[:,0],'PC2':scores[:,1]}).to_string(index=False,formatters={'PC1':'{:.3f}'.format,'PC2':'{:.3f}'.format}))\\n print('symbolic variable-score correlations')\\n corr=np.corrcoef(np.column_stack([A,scores]).T)[:len(names),len(names):]\\n print(pd.DataFrame(corr,index=names,columns=['PC1','PC2']).loc[sym].round(3).to_string())\\nPY\\npython /tmp/pcacheck.py\",\"timeout_seconds\":120}"

{

      "function": {
        "name": "execute_command",
        "arguments": "{\"summary\":\"Check native Z-score scaling and produce reproducible analysis script\",\"command\":\"python - <<'PY'\\nimport pandas as pd\\nD=pd.read_csv('data/capital.csv',dtype=str,keep_default_na=False)\\nX=D.iloc[:,3:].apply(lambda s: pd.to_numeric(s.str.replace(',','.',regex=False)))\\nprint('indicator means/std sample')\\nprint(pd.DataFrame({'mean':X.mean(),'sd':X.std(),'min':X.min(),'max':X.max()}).round(3).to_string())\\nprint('BCE groups',D.groupby('group').size().to_dict())\\nPY\\nmkdir -p analysis\\ncat > analysis/capital_pca.py <<'PY'\\n#!/usr/bin/env python3\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"Reproducible PCA on one capital.csv observation per 100-year BCE slice.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\nimport numpy as np\\nimport pandas as pd\\n\\nPATH = 'data/capital.csv'\\nSYMBOLIC = ['Zhausgr', 'ZDolch', 'ZAxtSchwert']\\ndf = pd.read_csv(PATH, dtype=str, keep_default_na=False)\\nindicators = list(df.columns[3:])\\n# Decimal comma is the CSV's decimal separator; don't use thousands grouping.\\nXdf = df[indicators].apply(lambda s: pd.to_numeric(s.str.strip().str.replace(',', '.', regex=False), errors='coerce'))\\nassert df.shape == (39, 15)\\nassert Xdf.notna().all().all()\\nbce = pd.to_numeric(df['BCE'], errors='raise').astype(int)\\nassert np.all(np.diff(bce.to_numpy()) == -100)\\n# Indicators are supplied as Z values. Center them for PCA but do not rescale\\n# their standard deviations; this is covariance PCA on the provided Z matrix.\\nA = Xdf.to_numpy(float) - Xdf.to_numpy(float).mean(axis=0)\\nU, singular, VT = np.linalg.svd(A, full_matrices=False)\\nV = VT[:2].T.copy()\\nscores = A @ V\\n# Fix arbitrary component signs for readable values (does not affect relationships).\\nif V[:, 0].sum() < 0:\\n    V[:, 0] *= -1; scores[:, 0] *= -1\\nj = np.argmax(np.abs(V[:, 1]))\\nif V[j, 1] < 0:\\n    V[:, 1] *= -1; scores[:, 1] *= -1\\nvariance = singular**2 / (len(A)-1)\\nratio = variance / variance.sum()\\nloadings = pd.DataFrame(V, index=indicators, columns=['PC1', 'PC2'])\\nscore_df = pd.DataFrame({'BCE': bce, 'PC1': scores[:, 0], 'PC2': scores[:, 1]})\\nprint('rows/columns:', df.shape, 'numeric indicator cells:', int(Xdf.notna().sum().sum()))\\nprint('BCE range and step:', (int(bce.min()), int(bce.max())), np.unique(np.diff(bce)))\\nprint('PCA: centered source Z variables (not re-standardized); variance explained:', ratio[:2])\\nprint('Loadings:')\\nprint(loadings.round(4).to_string())\\nprint('Symbolic variable PC1/PC2 loadings:')\\nprint(loadings.loc[SYMBOLIC].round(4).to_string())\\nprint('Century scores:')\\nprint(score_df.to_string(index=False, formatters={'PC1':'{:.3f}'.format, 'PC2':'{:.3f}'.format}))\\nPY\\npython analysis/capital_pca.py\",\"timeout_seconds\":120}"

<details>
<summary>Request message 1: System</summary>

````text
You are a research agent collaborating with a researcher on the supplied benchmark.
Keep its original question, fixed methods, constraints and required outputs unchanged.
First inspect inputs/repository and propose an approach. Before the main analysis or
implementation, yield a stage=plan handoff: describe observations, proposed choices,
assumptions and important uncertainties. Wait for explicit researcher approval.
Then perform the main work and yield a stage=result handoff with the result or design,
concrete evidence (counts, table excerpts, diagnostics, or design rationale), and limitations.
Use end_researcher_turn for every handoff. The same conversation and workspace persist.
Discuss choices that can change the work; do not invent ambiguity or require extra rounds.
Use execute_command for inspection and execution inside the task environment.

      "function": {
        "name": "execute_command",
        "arguments": "{\"summary\": \"Check native Z-score scaling and produce reproducible analysis script\", \"command\": \"python - <<'PY'\\nimport pandas as pd\\nD=pd.read_csv('data/capital.csv',dtype=str,keep_default_na=False)\\nX=D.iloc[:,3:].apply(lambda s: pd.to_numeric(s.str.replace(',','.',regex=False)))\\nprint('indicator means/std sample')\\nprint(pd.DataFrame({'mean':X.mean(),'sd':X.std(),'min':X.min(),'max':X.max()}).round(3).to_string())\\nprint('BCE groups',D.groupby('group').size().to_dict())\\nPY\\nmkdir -p analysis\\ncat > analysis/capital_pca.py <<'PY'\\n#!/usr/bin/env python3\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"Reproducible PCA on one capital.csv observation per 100-year BCE slice.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\nimport numpy as np\\nimport pandas as pd\\n\\nPATH = 'data/capital.csv'\\nSYMBOLIC = ['Zhausgr', 'ZDolch', 'ZAxtSchwert']\\ndf = pd.read_csv(PATH, dtype=str, keep_default_na=False)\\nindicators = list(df.columns[3:])\\n# Decimal comma is the CSV's decimal separator; don't use thousands grouping.\\nXdf = df[indicators].apply(lambda s: pd.to_numeric(s.str.strip().str.replace(',', '.', regex=False), errors='coerce'))\\nassert df.shape == (39, 15)\\nassert Xdf.notna().all().all()\\nbce = pd.to_numeric(df['BCE'], errors='raise').astype(int)\\nassert np.all(np.diff(bce.to_numpy()) == -100)\\n# Indicators are supplied as Z values. Center them for PCA but do not rescale\\n# their standard deviations; this is covariance PCA on the provided Z matrix.\\nA = Xdf.to_numpy(float) - Xdf.to_numpy(float).mean(axis=0)\\nU, singular, VT = np.linalg.svd(A, full_matrices=False)\\nV = VT[:2].T.copy()\\nscores = A @ V\\n# Fix arbitrary component signs for readable values (does not affect relationships).\\nif V[:, 0].sum() < 0:\\n    V[:, 0] *= -1; scores[:, 0] *= -1\\nj = np.argmax(np.abs(V[:, 1]))\\nif V[j, 1] < 0:\\n    V[:, 1] *= -1; scores[:, 1] *= -1\\nvariance = singular**2 / (len(A)-1)\\nratio = variance / variance.sum()\\nloadings = pd.DataFrame(V, index=indicators, columns=['PC1', 'PC2'])\\nscore_df = pd.DataFrame({'BCE': bce, 'PC1': scores[:, 0], 'PC2': scores[:, 1]})\\nprint('rows/columns:', df.shape, 'numeric indicator cells:', int(Xdf.notna().sum().sum()))\\nprint('BCE range and step:', (int(bce.min()), int(bce.max())), np.unique(np.diff(bce)))\\nprint('PCA: centered source Z variables (not re-standardized); variance explained:', ratio[:2])\\nprint('Loadings:')\\nprint(loadings.round(4).to_string())\\nprint('Symbolic variable PC1/PC2 loadings:')\\nprint(loadings.loc[SYMBOLIC].round(4).to_string())\\nprint('Century scores:')\\nprint(score_df.to_string(index=False, formatters={'PC1':'{:.3f}'.format, 'PC2':'{:.3f}'.format}))\\nPY\\npython analysis/capital_pca.py\", \"timeout_seconds\": 120}"

Thus they align with the positive side of PC1, but are moderate rather than the strongest positive PC1 indicators in this fit (e.g. copper/gold 0.399, pottery decoration 0.381). On PC2, the three are positive but not equally prominent: house size is clearly higher (**0.364**), while daggers (**0.120**) and especially hatchets/swords (**0.067**) lie much closer to zero. BCE spacing is uniformly 100 years. Symbolic capital consists of Hatchet & Swords, Daggers, House Size. 2. Social Capital consists of Copper and Gold, Amber, Monument Count 3. Cultural Capital consists of Diversity of Pottery form, Diversity of Pottery Decoration. 4. Economic Capital consists of Depot, Sickle, Axes & Celts, Monument Size. 5. Human impact or landscape openness, respectively, as reflected in the pollen data, can be used as a demographic indicator based on the assumption that an increasing population density leads to increasing woodland clearance due to an increasing demand for resources including wood, agricultural land and settlement areas. Each sample from the pollen record used in the principal component analysis is absolutely dated and therefore the openness score (PC 1.) can be plotted as a time series, expressing human induced land clearance. The 'Belau_PC1' of pollen data (pollen_openness_score_Belau_Woserin_Feeser_et_al_2019.csv) has been assumed to reflect openness. Original openness score through 'Belau_PC1', 100-year smoothed openness score, and linear interpolation of openness score have been used to signify growth. The original openness score (Belau_PC1), the 100-year smoothed openness score (pollen_inter_100), and the linear interpolation of the openness score (pollen_inter) have been used to signify openness. 6. Demographic growth manifests itself in growth set bringing with it a further opening of the landscape. Opening of landscape corresponds to higher growth rates. During the Early Neolithic, we are dealing with a population growth that goes hand in hand with the opening up of vegetation and the cultivation of the landscape. The growth rate is defined as the percentage change of the 100-year smoothed openness score (pollen_grate_100). 7.
